## [2026-09-25T12:18:06Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2023-06-30-objection-in-90-of-md-74d0930771 chunk 1 item 3 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000002: quote 'How about instead of asking YES-oriented questions like, "Would you be able to make a decision in one month?"Ask somethi'; exact source text 'We are so afraid to hear the truth about the potential obstacles and objections at the beginning, we end up rushing into: - The presentation - Sending property options - Trying to convince the client that this property and current market are the best After this, the client simply can’t be honest with us. How about instead of asking YES-oriented questions like, "Would you be able to make a decision in one month?" Ask something like, "In a scenario where we find the best property for you, you probably WILL NOT be able to make a decision within 1 week CORRECT?” Let him convince you - "Why not? I definitely will." Or, come up with the objections and discuss his real circumstances, "Well probably not, as I need to sell my wife’s diamond ring first…" You are qualifying, by disqualifying.'

## [2026-09-25T12:18:30Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2023-07-13-succeeding-as-a-new-real-md-72dc7089ed chunk 1 item 1 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000001: quote '...depends 70% on the belief system...'; exact source text "Succeeding as a new real estate agent in a market depends 70% on the belief system you have and the environment you are in. There are companies where, on paper, you can keep more of your commission, but in reality, you may not have much left to keep. My key point is that you should establish whether or not you can be productive in a company as a new agent. Here's how I would determine it if I were a brand new agent in the current market: 1) What are the character traits of the people who succeed in your team?"

One of my trainees asked me last week - what do I think of “luck” as a component for real estate success? ## [2026-09-25T15:14:51Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2023-08-22-i-support-the-idea-that-md-033fc90c4a chunk 1 item 2 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000001: quote "The Socratic sales method is more effective than the traditional 'hard sell' method for several reasons."; exact source text 'I support the idea that the best way to sell something is to help the customer discover their own needs and how your product or service can meet those needs. The Socratic sales method is more effective than the traditional "hard sell" method for several reasons. First, when you ask questions and listen carefully to the answers, you show the customer that you\'re interested in understanding their needs. This makes the customer more likely to trust you and be open to your suggestions. Second, the customer explores their own needs and options and builds a rapport with you at the same time. By asking open-ended questions, you can help the customer think through their needs and consider different solutions. This leads to better decision-making and a higher chance of closing the deal.'

## [2026-09-25T15:16:16Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2023-09-19-im-receiving-at-least-5-md-4a2793c63e chunk 1 item 5 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000002: quote '- Ask soft-opening questions. "Why did you choose this project?" Or start with… "I am curious. What was it about the ad '; exact source text 'The majority of agents completely ignore client discovery or make it feel like an interrogation. Because of this, clients hold onto their information and give vague answers. The result? A loss on both sides. How can you be different and fix this in your sales process? - Do not assume. Be curious about this particular client’s situation. - Ask soft-opening questions. “Why did you choose this project?” Or start with… “I am curious. What was it about the ad that attracted your attention?” - Clarify the intentions. “Let’s say, we found a property which matches your criteria XYZ, what would be your next possible steps?”'
## [2026-09-25T15:16:16Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2023-09-19-im-receiving-at-least-5-md-4a2793c63e chunk 1 item 7 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000003: quote 'Show that you care about their particular situation. "Help me to understand. When you said you want the property for bot'; exact source text '- Show that you care about their particular situation. “Help me to understand. When you said you want the property for both personal use and as an investment, what exactly do you mean by that?” The client will only choose to work with you if he has the feeling - that you are the one who understands his needs. Good luck!'

I got sick yesterday and set aside this time to catch up on all my business development activities. I\'ve sent 12 messages, 6 of which were video or audio. The main trick here is not to use phrases like "I\'m following up" or "checking in," as these can make you appear needy in the eyes of your clients. You\'ll likely receive a response in less than an hour!'

## [2026-09-25T15:21:44Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2024-01-23-how-to-become-a-trusted-md-ffb9afde45 chunk 1 item 1 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000001: quote '...I need to know more about your situation, such as...'; exact source text 'How to become a trusted Adviser for your Clients? Level 1. Authority doesn\'t solely come from highlighting past achievements. Rarely do agents come to a potential client saying, "I\'m #10 in the area." Everyone claims to be the best, and it can lead clients to view you as just another salesperson trying to sell me... Puff💨- the trust is Gone Level 2. A more effective approach is a neutral one, avoiding desperation in pitching yourself as #1. Instead, act as a confident professional, saying, "I\'ve successfully helped clients with similar requests to buy/sell properties with a high premium. However, that doesn\'t mean that what I do will 100% work for you. I need to know more about your situation, such as..." Begin with your qualification questions to truly understand the client\'s needs.'

Spoiler alert: it didn’t require a total overhaul—just some strategic tweaks. Before we could fix anything, we needed to figure out what was actually broken—because, as they say, if you don’t know what’s broken, you might end up fixing the wrong thing. So, we took a hard look at: The number of incoming leads. - How many of those leads were actually answering calls. - The percentage of calls that turned into potential clients. - How many clients were willing to move to the next stage—a Zoom call. - How many of those Zoom calls actually happened.'

## [2026-09-25T15:35:35Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2024-11-18-dont-get-mad-at-me-md-b921920af9 chunk 1 item 1 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000001: quote 'I don’t respond to messages like, "Hi, Olga. It’s been a long time since we connected. How have you been?"'; exact source text 'Don’t Get Mad at Me! I Won’t Respond to Generic Messages on LinkedIn Let’s be honest: LinkedIn is an incredible platform for networking. But it’s also a playground for messages that make you wonder, “Did they even read my name?” Here’s the deal — I don’t respond to messages like, “Hi, Olga. It’s been a long time since we connected. How have you been?” Why? Because these messages don’t require the sender to switch on their brain. They feel robotic, and honestly, if you’re not putting in the effort to engage meaningfully, why should I? If you truly want to connect with someone who can add value to your business, here’s my advice: 1. Take the Time to Read Their Profile Show me you know who I am beyond my job title. 2. Be Specific and Relevant'

Be Specific and Relevant'

About time."; exact source text 'LinkedIn Asked my Opinion on... "The announcement of Etihad Rail will impact real estate through.." my thoughts... With new connectivity across the UAE, we’re looking at previously untapped areas becoming prime real estate hotspots. Think about it—every major city in the world thrives on suburban living, and this is our moment to see that model take shape right here. Less traffic in central areas? Yes, please. More affordable housing options? Finally. New investment opportunities in areas that were once “too far”? About time. For years, certain parts of the UAE have been off the radar simply because getting there was a logistical nightmare. Etihad Rail changes that. It opens doors (and tracks) to new workforce hubs, suburban communities, and long-term real estate appreciation.'

## [2026-09-25T15:41:08Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2025-04-04-18-minutes-a-day-thats-md-dee5e3474d chunk 1 item 2 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000001: quote "Because most people quit after a few weeks. Get distracted. Tell themselves they’ll 'start fresh next month.'"; exact source text '18 minutes a day. That’s all it takes to outperform 95% of people in your field. Sounds too simple? Let me explain. In 1976, psychologist Anders Ericsson conducted a study to answer one powerful question: “How many hours of focused practice are needed to become better than 95% of people in any given profession?” The answer: Just 100 hours per year. That’s 18 minutes a day. And most people won’t do it. Because most people quit after a few weeks. Get distracted. Tell themselves they’ll “start fresh next month.” But the ones who win? They don’t hustle 24/7. They show up for 18 focused minutes a day. And they do it consistently. You don’t need to be the most talented. You just need to keep going longer than those who stop. If you’re still here — you’re already closer than you think.'
## [2026-09-25T15:41:08Z] ingest | linkedin-posts-2025-04-04-18-minutes-a-day-thats-md-dee5e3474d | 5 evidence records | 1 chunks
## [2026-09-25T15:41:17Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2025-04-07-lets-be-real-99-of-md-103eaf9d19 chunk 1 item 2 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000001: quote 'If you’re doing the same thing every agent does — sounding nervous, scripted, and salesy — of course you’re getting slap'; exact source text "Let’s be real: 99% of people are scared of making calls. Not just cold calls — any calls. And no, you're not broken. You just don't know the right mechanic. If you’re doing the same thing every agent does — sounding nervous, scripted, and salesy — of course you’re getting slapped with “I’m not interested.” But here’s the fun part: I’ve got one little script. Simple. Smooth. Designed for people who hate calling… and still want results. Drop a “+” if you want it."

And in real estate, you are the business. Whether you’re an agent, a sales manager, or a sales director — you’re not just working in sales. You’re running your own system, your own pipeline, your own performance engine. Most people track how many leads they bring in. They even track how many deals they close. But what happens in between those two points? Mystery. Magic. Bermuda Triangle. No visibility. No structure. No control. And that’s where most money gets lost. Time and time again, when I work with companies and review their sales processes, lead qualification, marketing, or recruitment — I see the same thing: They know performance is below expectations. But when I ask: “What’s the benchmark?” “What’s the actual conversion rate?”'

## [2026-09-25T15:43:32Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2025-05-14-how-to-follow-up-with-md-8866d75461 chunk 1 item 3 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000001: quote "Because staying 'top of mind' doesn’t mean being constantly in their inbox."; exact source text 'How to follow up with a seller (without looking like a stalker). Yes — this is a real issue. And if you’re in sales, you’ve definitely felt it. You want the listing. You’ve already followed up once. Maybe twice. Now you’re sitting there, second-guessing everything: — “Did I sound too eager?” — “Is it too soon to follow up again?” — “Should I ask about his weekend? His cat? Anything?” At some point, following up feels less like relationship-building… and more like digital lurking. I’ve been there. And honestly? It’s not just uncomfortable — it’s ineffective. Because staying “top of mind” doesn’t mean being constantly in their inbox. It means showing up strategically. With purpose. With clarity. With timing that makes sense for them, not just for you.'

Here\'s what happened next. While her colleagues were stealing leads behind each other\'s backs, she refused to play that game. "It\'s against my religious beliefs," she told her manager. Her team was toxic. Agents would call prospects already being handled by other agents. They\'d backstab each other for deals. The culture was "steal or starve." But she wouldn\'t do it. Her manager even praised her for being the ONLY person on the floor who didn\'t approach leads already in contact with other agents. "It\'s haram in my religion," she explained. "Even if you\'re not Muslim, I don\'t steal from people. Why would I do something I wouldn\'t want done to me?" So what happened to the "ethical" agent in Dubai\'s cutthroat market?'

Confidence closes deals. Desperation kills them. Are you selling from abundance or scarcity? PS. News, regional conflict and constant headlines are already affecting how buyers think and make decisions. So the real question for agents is simple: How do you keep making money in a war-affected market? On March 11 at 8:00 PM (Dubai time) Ashley and I will go live to talk about what real estate professionals should actually do right now. We’ll cover: • how to reconnect with clients who suddenly went quiet • what to say when a client tells you “the market will go down and Dubai is finished” • how to generate leads in uncertain times • how to keep deals moving when buyers go into wait mode 📍The live will be hosted on my YouTube channel. If you’re not following my YouTube yet — the link is in my bio. Join us.'

## [2026-09-27T12:08:06Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2026-04-20-i-almost-gave-up-on-md-65c086757d chunk 1 item 5 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000002: quote "That was my challenge - I couldn’t not 'convince' - Clients were not listening to me!"; exact source text 'That was my challenge - I couldn’t not “convince “ - Clients were not listening to me! And I was too - proud- I’ve seen others begging, chasing, being desperate I could not beg… too proud. So I had two choices. Quit, buy a ticket, and go back home. Or stay and figure out a different way… I stayed. It did not happen overnight. But when I changed the way I spoke to clients, everything started to change. My script changed. My confidence changed. My sales changed. That is why I still believe this today: In a difficult market, your ability to influence people on the phone can change everything. Not through pressure. Not through pushing or beginning But through the kind of conversation that builds trust and helps people move forward.'

## [2026-09-27T12:09:20Z] rejected-item | linkedin-posts-2026-07-13-in-20-years-you-would-md-07336225bc chunk 1 item 1 | Unsupported excerpt or unit ID u000001: quote 'Gratitude is not about pretending that'; exact source text '“In 20 years, you would give everything to be where you are right now. At this age, in this body, living this exact life.” Jimmy Carr shared this thought on The Diary of a CEO, and it genuinely stayed with me. It’s when you say it like this… it hits. You might miss your current age, your health, your body, the people around you and even the problems that now feel so important. It immediately brings you back to the present. But then I decided to reverse the question. How much would I pay to return to my life 20 years ago, when I was 26? Or even 10 years ago, when I was 36? Honestly? Nothing. Not because those years were bad. They were necessary. They shaped me, taught me and brought me here. But I do not want my old life back. I want this version of my life.'
